(B.20)

\displaystyle\left|\mu_{\varepsilon}\right|(B_{2\rho}(0))

\displaystyle=2\pi\sum_{K\in\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}}\left|\deg(v_{\varepsilon},\,\partial K)\right|\leq C

(B.21)

\displaystyle\left|\mu_{\varepsilon}\right|(B_{2\rho}(0)\setminus\bar{B}_{\rho}(0))

\displaystyle=2\pi\sum_{K\in\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}\colon K\subseteq B_{2\rho}(0)\setminus B_{\rho}(0)}\left|\deg(v_{\varepsilon},\,\partial K)\right|\leq C\delta

Step 4

(Convergence of
\mu_{\varepsilon}
)
.

We claim that

(B.22)

\mu_{\varepsilon}\to 2\pi d_{j}\delta_{0}\qquad\textrm{in }W^{-1,1}(B_{2\rho}(0))\quad\textrm{as }\varepsilon\to 0.

Let
\varphi\in W^{1,\infty}_{0}(B_{2\rho}(0))
be a test function.
We have

(B.23)

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}(0)}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\jmath}(v_{\varepsilon})-\int_{B_{2\rho}(0)}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varepsilon}\right|\\
&\qquad\leq\underbrace{\left|\sum_{K\in\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{K}(\varphi-\varphi(b_{K}))\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\jmath}(v_{\varepsilon})\right|}_{=:I_{1}}+\underbrace{\left|\sum_{K\in\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi(b_{K})\left(\int_{K}\mathrm{d}\bar{\jmath}(v_{\varepsilon})-2\pi\deg(v_{\varepsilon},\,\partial K)\right)\right|}_{=:I_{2}}\end{split}

To estimate the term
I_{1}
, we observe that

\left|\mathrm{d}\bar{\jmath}(v_{\varepsilon})\right|\leq\left|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\leq 2\bar{e}_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})
and hence,

(B.24)

\begin{split}I_{1}\leq Ch(\varepsilon)\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\rho}(0))}\int_{B_{2\rho}(0)}\bar{e}_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ubH11-1}}}{{\leq}}Ch(\varepsilon)\left|\log\varepsilon\right|\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\rho}(0))}\end{split}

We have
h(\varepsilon)\left|\log\varepsilon\right|=\left|\log\varepsilon\right|^{-1}\to 0

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. To estimate
I_{2}
, we apply Stokes’ theorem
and (
2.64
):

\begin{split}I_{2}\leq\left|\sum_{K\in\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial K}\bar{\jmath}(v_{\varepsilon})\left(1-\frac{1}{\left|v_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}\right)\right|\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\rho}(0))}\end{split}

The
L^{\infty}
-norm of
1-\left|v_{\varepsilon}\right|^{-2}
on
R_{\varepsilon}

can be bounded from below by (
B.16
). Moreover, we have

\left|\bar{\jmath}(v_{\varepsilon})\right|\leq\left|v_{\varepsilon}\right|\left|\nabla v_{\varepsilon}\right|

and, since
\left|v_{\varepsilon}\right|
is bounded on
R_{\varepsilon}
by (
B.16
), we obtain

\begin{split}I_{2}&\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{1/2}\left|\log\varepsilon\right|^{1/2}}{h(\varepsilon)^{1/2}}\left(\int_{R_{\varepsilon}}\bar{e}_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\mathscr{H}^{1}\right)\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\rho}(0))}\\
&\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{1/2}\left|\log\varepsilon\right|^{1/2}}{h(\varepsilon)^{1/2}}\left(\int_{R_{\varepsilon}}\bar{e}_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})\mathrm{d}\mathscr{H}^{1}\right)^{1/2}\mathscr{H}^{1}(R_{\varepsilon})^{1/2}\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\rho}(0))}\end{split}

By construction,
\mathscr{H}^{1}(R_{\varepsilon})\leq Ch(\varepsilon)^{-1}
.
By applying (
B.10
), we deduce

(B.25)

\begin{split}I_{2}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{1/2}}{h(\varepsilon)^{3/2}}\left|\log\varepsilon\right|\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\rho}(0))}=C\varepsilon^{1/2}\left|\log\varepsilon\right|^{4}\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\rho}(0))}\end{split}

Combining (
B.23
) with (
B.24
), (
B.25
),
we obtain

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}(0)}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\jmath}(v_{\varepsilon})-\int_{B_{2\rho}(0)}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varepsilon}\right|\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq C\left(\left|\log\varepsilon\right|^{-1}+\varepsilon^{1/2}\left|\log\varepsilon\right|^{4}\right)\left(\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\rho}(0))}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\rho}(0))}\right)\end{split}

that is,
\mathrm{d}\bar{\jmath}(v_{\varepsilon})-\mu_{\varepsilon}\to 0
strongly
in
W^{-1,1}(B_{2\rho}(0))
. Now (
B.22
)
follows, due to (
B.9
).

Step 5

(Conclusion)
.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(B_{2\rho}(0)
be such that

0\leq\psi\leq 1
and
\psi=1
in
B_{\rho}(0)
. By
construction of the measure
\mu_{\varepsilon}
(see (
B.17
))
and additivity of the degree, we have

(B.26)

\begin{split}2\pi\deg(v_{\varepsilon},\,\partial B_{\rho}(0))=\mu_{\varepsilon}(B_{\rho}(0))=\int_{B_{2\rho}(0)}\psi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varepsilon}-\int_{B_{2\rho}(0)\setminus\bar{B}_{\rho}(0)}\psi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varepsilon}\end{split}

By (
B.22
), we have

(B.27)

\begin{split}\int_{B_{2\rho}(0)}\psi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varepsilon}\to 2\pi d_{j}\psi(0)=2\pi d_{j}\qquad\textrm{as }\varepsilon\to 0.\end{split}

On the other hand,

(B.28)

\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}(0)\setminus\bar{B}_{\rho}(0)}\psi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varepsilon}\right|\leq\left|\mu_{\varepsilon}\right|(B_{2\rho}(0)\setminus\bar{B}_{\rho}(0))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ubH13-6}}}{{\leq}}C\delta

Therefore, for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small we have

\left|\deg(v_{\varepsilon},\,\partial B_{\rho}(0))-d_{j}\right|\leq C\delta

We choose
\delta
small enough, so that
C\delta<1
.
Since both
\deg(v_{\varepsilon},\,\partial B_{\rho}(0))
and
d_{j}

are integers, we conclude that
\deg(v_{\varepsilon},\,\partial B_{\rho}(0))=d_{j}

for
\varepsilon
small enough, and the lemma follows. ∎
